#2238. Hearts reveal investment in push for European return resulted in £2.4m loss

Hearts have announced a loss of £2.4million after investing heavily to return to European football this season.

The Tynecastle club backed Derek McInnes after he took over as boss in the summer of 2025.

The Jambos’ thrilling William Hill Premiership title challenge went to the last day of the season when they lost to Celtic at Parkhead to finish second to the Hoops.

Hearts lost to Sturm Graz in the Champions League qualifiers and then to Benfica in the Europa League, but victory over Rapid Vienna saw Wouter Vrancken’s side make it to the league phase of the Conference League, where they face Swiss side Thun on Thursday.

Hearts also reported a turnover of £23.1m for the 2025/26 financial year, are also in “positive” discussions about further investment to help Vrancken in the January transfer window and are confident of a “significantly improved financial result” in a year’s time.

Derek McInnes on Findlay Curtis Rangers future as he reveals player stance The Celtic youngster Martin O'Neill was keen to check on during Ross County clash

The club’s financial results also revealed that staff costs went up eight per cent “with player acquisition costs of £7.8m across the year, reflecting significant investment in the playing squad”.

A statement on Hearts’ official website said: “The club took a deliberate decision to invest in the playing squad from a position of financial strength, helping secure a return to European league phase football for 2026/27.

“This additional investment in squad wages, combined with the absence of European income, resulted in an operating loss of £2.4m for the year.

“With European income returning and further progress in player trading, the club anticipates a significantly improved financial result in 2026/27.

“Strong underlying revenue growth offset much of the loss of European income for the 2025/26 financial year, with sponsorship and advertising income up 37 per cent and retail and hospitality income up 17 per cent, reflecting continued commercial progress and support from partners and fans alike.

“The club continued to benefit from exceptional financial backing from its benefactors and Foundation of Hearts members, and extends its sincere thanks for their continued commitment and support.

“The increased investment in the playing squad and football operations was supported by an investment from Tony Bloom towards the end of the previous financial year, which strengthened the club’s financial position and provided capacity to invest in improving the playing squad.

“As an equity investment, this funding is reflected in the balance sheet rather than recognised as income in the profit and loss account.

“The reported operating loss of £2.4m includes £1.8m of gains from player trading and is stated before depreciation, amortisation and finance costs.

“Further gains will be reflected in the 2026/27 financial year following player trading activity completed in July and August 2026.

“The club is also engaged in positive discussions regarding further investment to support the continued, measured pursuit of the club’s ambitions.

“The club’s balance sheet remains robust, with the accounts reporting net assets of £20.3m at the June 30 2026 financial year-end.”

Per Paolo Ghiglione potrebbe esserci una nuova vita dopo l’esonero di Serse Cosmi e l’arrivo in panchina di Vito Di Bari alla Salernitana. L’esperto terzino infatti finora è rimasto ai margini della squadra non venendo mai convocato nelle prime dodici gare di campionato, ma adesso la musica potrebbe cambiare.

Un reintegro nelle prossime settimane potrebbe aprire nuove prospettive al difensore. #2236. ‘I hold my hands up’ - McAvennie issues fresh apology to Celtic star Auston Trusty

Frank McAvennie has issued a full apology to Auston Trusty and his family after admitting his initial response to criticism over comments about the Celtic defender “didn’t go far enough”.

The former Celtic striker faced an online backlash after questioning the length of time Trusty had been away from football following the death of his father.

McAvennie has now returned to the issue and said he is “genuinely sorry” for any hurt caused by his remarks.

In a fresh apology, he said: “Frank here. I’ve had more time to think about what I said about Auston on the podcast and I know my statement yesterday didn’t go far enough.

“I’m genuinely sorry for any hurt I’ve caused Auston or his family. That’s the last thing I’d ever want.

“I hold my hands up and I’ve learned my lesson.

“I genuinely wish Auston and his family all the very best.”

McAvennie's original comments came on his Let Me Be Frank podcast as he discussed Trusty's absence from the Celtic team.

He said: “I’ve got every respect for that, but how long do you want off?

“Martin (O’Neill) says he’s away to celebrate the life of his dad.

Hearts reveal investment in push for European return resulted in £2.4m loss Derek McInnes on Findlay Curtis Rangers future as he reveals player stance

“Celebrate the life? You’ve got football. You’re getting paid and we’re in trouble. How long is it going to be?

“It’s nonsense. My dad was the best man, the only man I looked up to, when he died.

Following criticism of those remarks, McAvennie initially acknowledged that he should have chosen his words more carefully.

He said: “I can understand why people feel my comments were insensitive, and looking back, I should have chosen my words more carefully.

“Everyone deals with grief differently, and I certainly meant no disrespect to Auston or his family.

“Celtic need the big man back, and that’s what I was clumsily trying to say.

“I wish him and his family all the very best.”

McAvennie has now conceded that statement did not adequately address his original remarks and offered a more direct apology to the Celtic defender and his family.

#2235. Hearts get bad news on Calvin Miller in latest injury blow

Hearts boss Wouter Vrancken revealed Calvin Miller will require surgery on a knee injury that will keep him out for two months.

Both Tomas Magnusson and Miller came off during the first half of the Jambos’ 2-1 Premier Sports Cup semi-final defeat to Edinburgh rivals Hibernian at Barclays Hampden on Sunday.

The Iceland midfielder was taken to hospital with concussion and the former Celtic, Morton and Falkirk attacker left the national stadium on crutches and his knee in a brace.

Vrancken, who will be without 10 first-team players for the Conference League game against FC Thun on Thursday in Switzerland, was asked about Miller and said: “It will be two months, let’s say, depending on what the surgery says because it’s a meniscus injury.

“Tomas is doing good. The day after was already a lot better.

“There is a protocol, of course, with concussions. He will be for sure back after the (international) break.”

Vrancken will have to put together a side capable of getting a first win in the competition, after a 1-1 home draw against Nordsjaelland and a 2-0 defeat away to Trabzonspor.

‘I hold my hands up’ - McAvennie issues fresh apology to Celtic star Auston Trusty Hearts reveal investment in push for European return resulted in £2.4m loss

He said: “Yes, it’s a puzzle. I’m always happy to see that the players we work with and play out of position or talk with are always directly motivated to do it.

“They know how much they help the team by doing it.

“It’s good to see because they take responsibility and they don’t live by excuses.

“They have already done it for weeks. That’s how we also get good results and good performances.

“Now we have to try to do it two more times before the break.”

Vrancken is confident his side will respond positively to their defeat by Hibs in Glasgow.

He said: “I think so. They showed already a few times this season that they can bounce back in performance.

“I think when you leave out the last 20-25 minutes of the first half, we played a good game.

“It’s just a pity that we couldn’t reward ourselves in the second half with a goal that could spin it around.

“But I’m confident in the players that they are mentally ready to play the game.

“They (Thun) have a good team, they have a lot of energy, they have a lot of intensity. They have direct play, going fast forward a lot of moments.

“With a lot of energy, good quality players also in front.

“We will have to be spot on, we have to be on our level to make the result. But the aim is to try to get a win here.”

#2234. Manchester United confirmed lineup: Amad & Yoro return to starting lineup

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur return from the international break with plenty to prove when they meet at Old Trafford on Saturday.

Michael Carrick’s side have won just once in their last four domestic matches since beating Ipswich 5-2. A Carabao Cup defeat to Brighton was followed by a loss to 10-man Manchester City, before Manchester United drew with Everton and Fulham. Matheus Cunha’s late equaliser at Fulham rescued a point, but Manchester United still sit 12th.

Roberto De Zerbi’s side are bottom of the Premier League with only two points from their opening five games. Spurs have failed to score in four of those matches, while defeats against Aston Villa and Newcastle have added to the pressure.

Michael Carrick has named his starting eleven for the clash at Old Trafford, with Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans partnering in midfield as United look to get their season back on track.

Senne Lammens starts in goal, with Leny Yoro, Lisandro Martinez, Harry Maguire and Luke Shaw forming the back four. Carrick has opted for Mainoo and Tielemans in the middle, with Amad Diallo, Bruno Fernandes and Matheus Cunha tasked with supporting Bryan Mbeumo up front.

Leny Yoro had a brilliant international break where he made his debut for France. His performance on debut was enough for Zidane to praise the debutant.

Amad, after missing the start of the season with an injury, returns straightaway to the team.

Carlos Baleba starts on the bench and is expected to make his debut today. Marcus Rashford is also on the bench alongside Shea Lacey.

Lammens; Yoro, Martinez, Maguire, Shaw; Mainoo, Tielemans; Amad, Bruno Fernandes, Mbeumo; Cunha.

Mbeumo gets his place through the middle, while Amad and Cunha start on the wing after scoring the late equaliser against Fulham. Fernandes will be expected to start producing the numbers from last season after a lackluster start to the season, except against Ipswich, before the international break.

United need a response after a difficult run, but Tottenham arrive under pressure of their own. With both sides desperate for points, the starting lineups set the stage for an important afternoon at Old Trafford.

#2233. Manchester United player ratings: United failed to win despite advantage

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur returned from the international break with plenty to prove after a disastrous start to the season.

United arrived with just one win, while Tottenham came to Old Trafford without a win.

Both teams had half-chances in the first half but little to show for them. Tottenham had the better chances with chances for Dominic Solanke, Connor Gallagher and Kudus, but none could take advantage of them.

Manchester United came close in the first half to score after Bryan Mbeumo raced towards the goal on a counter-attack to find Amad. He had a difficult shot, and eventually the shot was too weak to cause any damage. The first-half display was met by boos from the Old Trafford crowd at half-time and during the first half as well.

Solanke had another chance at the start of the second half but put it over the top, amid a wrestle with Lisandro Martinez in the six-yard box. It was United, however, who started the second half with promise.

Substitutions by Michael Carrick around the hour mark made the difference as United showed intent before Mbeumo got the goal off Cunha’s low cross. Luke Shaw was at the end of a scuffle with Bentancur, who was shown a red card for headbutting Shaw.

United failed to capitalize on their one-man advantage and seemed to sit on their 1-0 lead, but Solanke punished them with a header to level the game in injury time. The draw left United with just 6 points off their first six games. The result leaves United with their worst start in 40 years.

Lenne Sammens-6          Quiet afternoon for United no.1 till the goal, where he saw the ball go into the back of the net.

Leny Yoro-6       Defensively was fine but offered little upfront. Played out of position, and he showed good defensive awareness on the right back till his substitution.

Harry Maguire-5.5            Was decent in the first half but wasn’t much of an answer to Tottenham going forward in the second half

Lisandro Martinez-5      Took his eyes off the ball, resulting in conceding a late goal off Solanke.

Luke Shaw-6      Went up high on the pitch and remained a threat with his overlapping threat. Was involved in the scuffle that got Bentancur a red card for headbutting.

Kobbie Mainoo-6.5        Brilliant defensive tackle to deny Tottenham in the 68th minute. Remained composed in tight spaces against a physical Tottenham midfield.

Youri Tielemans-6          A decent performance and was solid throughout the minutes he was on the field

Bruno Fernandes-5        Another quiet and disappointing evening for the United captain after he failed to impress on a day he needed to get a result for United.

Amad-6              A positive return from injury, showed plenty of promise on the wing but failed to get past a resolute Tottenham side

Matheus Cunha-7           One of the liveliest options for United going forward. Provided an assist to Mbeumo to put United up front

Bryan Mbeumo-7.5        A brilliant finish off Matheus Cunha’s low ball to put United in front. Was dangerous going forward and denied a second on a counter by Kinsky off a brilliant long ball by Baleba.

Carlos Baleba (58’)-6.5  A decent debut overall for the Cameroon international. He put in a couple of dangerous balls up the field, including a clear ball to Mbeumo on a counter, who was denied by Kinsky.

Diogo Dalot (58’)-6      Provided a decent attacking threat when brought on around the hour mark.

Joshua Zirkzee (58’)-6   Showed plenty of promise and was a threat after he was brought in around the hour mark.

#2232. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2231. Liverpool lead twice before losing to Fenerbahce in Champions League

Even Liverpool’s multi-million pound bench could not rescue a team floundering in Istanbul as a chaotic performance was punished by Fenerbahce as they twice came from behind to win 3-2.

Alexander Isak had scored within three minutes of coming on to put the visitors ahead going into the final quarter but, like in the first half, the lead lasted just four minutes before former West Brom trainee Archie Brown equalised courtesy of some lax defending.

But worse was to follow as Vedat Muriqi outjumped three players to nod in the winner and end Liverpool’s 100 per cent record.

And while Andoni Iraola will be scrutinised for making five changes, including resting three of his front four in Sunday’s defeat to Arsenal, it was the way the team lost their cool and control in a game they had in hand which was more damaging.

It was a harsh result on 19-year-old Trey Nyoni, who belied his relative inexperience with a composed performance capped by an excellent volley to open the scoring, becoming Liverpool’s youngest player to register his maiden goal in the Champions League and second-youngest scorer in the competition after Trent Alexander-Arnold.

But Liverpool’s Turkish troubles continued – they lost twice to Galatasaray last season – on a typically fractious night in Istanbul, with former Manchester United forward Mason Greenwood scoring the first equaliser with a free-kick which deflected in off Jeremy Jacquet.

They had been in total command up to that point but from there the game descended into something more manic, which suited a team hammered 6-0 at Villa Park last month and stoked a febrile home crowd.

Hearts get bad news on Calvin Miller in latest injury blow ‘I hold my hands up’ - McAvennie issues fresh apology to Celtic star Auston Trusty

Many of Liverpool’s players did not cope well in that atmosphere but, to his immense credit, Nyoni shone.

Iraola’s choice to leave out Isak, Florian Wirtz and Cody Gakpo as they come to the end of a run of nine games in 27 days was understandable but it left his side toothless.

Bradley Barcola was deployed as striker but his unfamiliarity with the role was evident after failing to properly anticipate Joe Gomez’s low cross to the near post.

He did flick one header wide but struggled with the physicality of Fenerbahce’s centre-backs, even though Kojo Oppong was making his first Champions League start.

There was a lot of naivety within the visitors’ ranks – Victor Munoz snatched at an early chance when he was found in acres of space before redeeming himself by eventually forcing Ederson into a save – but none of it came from Nyoni.

The teenager was composed on just his seventh Champions League appearance – with everything going through him at the base of midfield.

His use of the ball, tactical awareness and unshakeable belief in his own ability meant amid the hurly burly of a typically Turkish clash he was head and shoulders above everyone else.

Even the usually unflappable Virgil van Dijk’s patience was tested, pushing Irfan Kahveci to the floor at a second-half corner, having twice had Romelu Lukaku leave something on him in the early stages.

When Liverpool’s breakthrough came it was fully deserved as Rio Ngumoha’s cross to the far post saw Van Dijk’s pressure force Brown to head weakly to the edge of the penalty area where Nyoni dispatched the dropping ball via a deflection off Oppong.

But after a sliding Kahveci smashed against the crossbar from five yards, Greenwood’s free-kick went through the wall.

Isak headed in Gakpo’s cross with his first chance after those two and Wirtz were summoned in the 66th minute, but the unmarked Brown tapped in the equaliser and more poor defending saw Muriqi head home the winner.

#2230. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2229. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2228. Celtic boss Martin O'Neill responds to Frank McAvennie's Auston Trusty remarks

Celtic manager Martin O'Neill has criticised comments made by former Parkhead forward Frank McAvennie about Auston Trusty following the sad passing of his father.

McAvennie questioned the amount of time the American had taken off to celebrate the life of his dad, before apologising via a statement on X.

Speaking ahead of Celtic's Europa League clash with Besiktas, O'Neill responded to McAvennie's initial remarks.

The 74-year-old explained that Trusty had requested leave and that he is with the defender 'the whole way'.

"That's really poor taste," O'Neill said of McAvennie's comments. "I know I've said some things and ended up backtracking. But that's a family bereavement.

"And that's making someone's mind up about how long they should be grieving and stuff like this here. That's not on.

"Trusty asked me for some time [off]. Even if it's a celebration [of his late father’s life], it doesn't matter. The father's been in poor health for quite some considerable time.

"And I know there was a stage where he thought that he wasn't going to see it through. But I'm with Trusty the whole way there. Because I wouldn't want anybody to tell me that I had to grieve over a brother or sister dying. And I've had that.

"He's 28 years of age. And he's taken it badly. Honestly. I've afforded him all the time he wants.

"At some stage, obviously, he'll be coming back. But at the end of the day, I've given that time because I know that we might be talking about different eras or whatever the case may be. That's nothing to do with it. This is a family thing. And that's not right.

"So, Trusty has got the time and he's got the backing of myself and he's got the backing of the club."

#2227. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2226. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

#2225. Aggie Beever-Jones gives Chelsea fresh encouragement as TWO moments show her value beyond goals

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Aggie Beever-Jones offered Chelsea encouragement after her England omission, contributing to two dangerous attacks during Sunday’s 3-0 victory at West Ham.

The forward replaced goalscorer Melvine Malard after 65 minutes, with Alyssa Thompson also entering as Sonia Bompastor refreshed her attack.

Chelsea led by one goal. The substitutes helped turn that narrow advantage into a comfortable finish.

🗣️ Sonia on Aggie’s form: “In these moments, it’s important for Aggie to keep working hard. There are not a lot of things she can control in this situation, so she needs to keep being confident, working hard on the pitch in training sessions, and we are here to support her in… pic.twitter.com/EktmCTtkhY

As Chelsea’s official match report details, Beever-Jones drove down the right and found Wieke Kaptein during the move that ended with Walsh striking the post.

She later supplied the final pass for Hamano’s goal.

Thompson’s shot drew a save, and Beever-Jones collected the loose ball before directing it towards Hamano, who headed home at the far post.

Those contributions showed two ways she can help Chelsea: carrying attacks forward and finding a team-mate inside the penalty area.

Thompson’s influence also deserves recognition. Both substitutes earned 8/10 in our Chelsea player ratings against West Ham.

Bompastor had already outlined Chelsea’s plan to help Beever-Jones work towards an England return after she missed the latest squad.

One productive appearance cannot settle questions about form or selection. It does give the striker something positive to build on.

Malard’s opener underlined the competition for her position. Beever-Jones responded by helping others threaten the goal, showing why her contribution deserves attention even when she does not score.

#2224. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2223. Wow' - Wolves star Adam Armstrong drops reaction after big personal moment

Wolverhampton Wanderers striker Adam Armstrong has taken to social media after recording his 500th career appearance in his side's 2-1 victory over Bristol City at Ashton Gate on Wednesday night.

The experienced striker has played for a plethora of clubs in the Premier League, Championship and League One, but for now is fully focused on aiding Wolves' aim of an instant return to the top-flight under Cesar Peixoto.

Armstrong played 85 of the 90 minutes in the West Country before being replaced by Tawanda Chirewa, just three minutes after his initial effort, which was saved by Brad Collins, was turned home on the rebound by Newcastle United loanee, Sean Neave, who went on to net the winner in the final minute.

It was a joyous occasion for all associated with the Black Country outfit, as a fourth win in five matches saw them climb into the second tier's automatic promotion places, just one point behind leaders Middlesbrough ahead of Saturday's trip to Wrexham.

Armstrong signed for his current employers in January from Southampton in a deal reportedly worth £7m plus £2m in add-ons.

The experienced forward has made 15 of his 500 appearances in the early weeks of the current campaign, during which he has scored four times, and will be hoping it ends in similar fashion to the 2023/24 season when he tasted promotion under Russell Martin at St Mary's.

Taking to Instagram after noting his personal milestone, the North East native said: "Wow what a night and what a win!!

"Who would have thought, 500. To the fans, teammates, staff at all the clubs. You played a huge part in me getting to this milestone.

"To my family. Thanks for all the support along the way. You are what drives me each day. Next goal promotion and 600 games," he added.

Among those in the replies paying tribute to the longevity of the striker's career were Ben Brereton Diaz, Will Smallbone, Harry Wilson, Bradley Dack and his current Molineux teammates, Mateus Mane and Tommy Doyle.

The Newcastle United academy graduate has enjoyed a fine first 500 games, with 102 of those coming in the Premier League, albeit he would have ultimately liked to have seen a better return than eight goals and nine assists in the top-flight.

However, Armstrong's clinical ability in front of goal hasn't ever been disputed in the Championship for the likes of Wolves, Southampton and Blackburn Rovers in particular.

Where does Adam Armstrong rank among the Championship's best strikers? Comment below!

289 of his outings have come in the division which he currently plies his trade in, and so have 94 of his goals and 37 of his assists, none bigger than his winner for Saints in the 2024 play-off final against Leeds United at Wembley Stadium.

Armstrong has started in nine of his 12 Championship outings so far this season, but it's fair to say the striker hadn't had the greatest of starts to the new season until recently.

He has since scored two in Wolves' past three games whilst having a big role in Neave's initial equaliser midweek, and this comes after receiving plenty of backing from Peixoto to start firing plenty of goals in.

The Newcastle United loanee's late brace ensured a vital three points for the Old Gold

“It is important for his confidence but he is an experienced player and I don’t believe he was losing confidence," said the Portuguese.

With Armstrong setting his dream out in terms of promotion, he will hope to start his next century of games with a positive result in North Wales this weekend.

#2222. Celtic AGM latest as O'Neill talks long-term future and tense CSC visit

For Martin O'Neill, it has already been a hectic week. On Monday evening, he attended a meeting at Greenock Celtic Supporters' Club alongside interim chairman Brian Wilson, chief executive Michael Nicholson, and chief financial officer Chris McKay. Over 120 fans were present as the Celtic manager and board faced, according to the club, 'hard but fair' questioning.

Less than 24 hours later, O'Neill found himself in Dingwall with assistant coaches Mark Fotheringham and Stephen McManus watching Celtic B lose 2-0 to Ross County in the Scottish Challenge Cup.

That's all before two crucial home fixtures against Besiktas in the Europa League and Kilmarnock in the William Hill Premiership come into the fray.

The 74-year-old sat down ahead of the tie with Besiktas to discuss Monday's meeting, his long-term future, and the club's upcoming Annual General Meeting (AGM).

"First of all, I wasn't really aware of the meeting, so it was kind of an ad hoc thing," O'Neill explained. "But I obviously was around. I spoke to Michael [Nicholson] and he said that they were going to meet.

"And I said, well, I wouldn't mind going down to it if that was the case, obviously to experience what the fans would be asking.

"The meeting itself was, I think you could use the word robust, yes, firm, but very orderly questioning.

"And trying to give the answers that, not necessarily what people are looking for, because I'm not so sure that will ever reach a satisfactory stage for a while anyway. But, yeah, it was really interesting and an experience.

"I probably didn't really need to go to a meeting, but these people were big Celtic fans. And naturally, I think both board and supporters, I thought there was a wanting the best for the football club.

"At the end of the day, there are bad times and I think that you should face up to that. Was everything answered by the board and myself to their satisfaction? One, you'd have to ask the people there at the time what was there.

"There was no shouting. It wasn't like the AGM that appeared [last year]. The shouting had taken place before the meeting."

Indeed, as O'Neill and company arrived in Greenock, a number of Celtic supporters wearing balaclavas were outside the supporters' club, voicing their own concerns.

"To me, I think that was the saddest thing," O'Neill continued. "Would I have ever, in any day, ever thought that Celtic fans would be clothed in balaclavas before a meeting? Even if they were disapproving of what was happening. I think that's really disappointing.

"I do find it hard to get my head round that kind of stuff.

"I think if you've got a dispute or something, I think you should be brave enough to show your face doing it.

"[It was] just on the way in. I don't know whether they expected me to be there or not, but it doesn't really matter. It was just regardless of who was there."

The hope is that this year's AGM doesn't entail similar hostilities. The last iteration was adjourned early following animosity between the hierarchy and supporters.

It's understood the 2026 AGM will take place in early December in person.

"I would expect to be at it as a manager," O'Neill said. "So if given an opportunity to answer some of the things and if they want to hear from the manager, I would have no problem.

"I hope it's similar to what Monday night was in terms of the questions and fairness and not falling into what it was last year."

As O'Neill discusses myriad off-pitch matters, it's easy to forget he's Celtic's football manager.

"People are asking me now if it’s something I’m going to continue to do," he added. "No, my main job depends on results and winning some football matches. That’s the most important thing."

Contracted at Celtic Park until the end of this season, O'Neill's deal has the option of a further 12 months.

This time last year, he was two games into what was meant to be the most fleeting of interim spells.

Wilfried Nancy's disastrous reign came and went and O'Neill returned in January, winning the double and staying on as permanent manager in the summer.

When asked if this was his final season in charge, O'Neill responded: "I can’t imagine anybody asking me back. Seriously, I can’t imagine that happening.

"Listen, I didn’t come in here today to give a final word at this minute."

If the coming months are to be his last, how important is it to go out on a high?

"It would be nice to see the club that I knew intimately from 2000 – having followed from a distance since my school days – be successful.

"Did I ever think I’d see a day when 75,000 people would descend on a UEFA Cup final day, with no arrests? There are still pictures on the wall here and all you see is a sea of green and white. To see that again, that would be your dream."

Celtic, though, could not be further away from reaching another European final. Sunday's defeat to Falkirk was their sixth in 10 matches, with Besiktas up next in the Europa League.

From their first three fixtures in the competition, Celtic are pointless, heightening criticism on O'Neill and his coaches.

"Everyone is a tactician now, that’s the whole point," he said. "Everybody is a football manager. There are people who have got jobs in football now – at football clubs – because they played PlayStation Manager.

"Honestly, it’s extraordinary. But it does not bother me, honestly, it doesn’t. People talk about tactics but in my semi-defence, no-one talked about tactics when we won the seven games to win the league [last season]. Or the 14 games we won before we got beat.

"And you know what, you’re right to think – if you got beaten in a game – that maybe somebody has out-thought you. Absolutely. You’re right to think that.

"[Falkirk manager] John McGlynn has every right to be proud of his side after beating us and to have had the upper hand."

Despite recent form, O'Neill remains more determined than ever to turn things around.

"I'm supposed to be the driver of the club," he quipped.

That said, the 74-year-old is well aware of the threat Besiktas, who sit eighth in the 36-team Europa League table, pose.

"I think these games are really hard for us," O'Neill said. "I think there are teams in the Europa League who are capable of playing in the Champions League and getting results in the Champions League at this minute. However, we're in the Europa League.

"We haven't got a point on the board. We're going to try and do something about it if we can on Thursday and then ask the question.

"But I think that no-one has ever lost the first three and qualified for the competition. So, we’ll be up against it. We're definitely up against it. But are we capable? Yeah, I have to believe that."
